Appendix C
Complements on strategies and valuations

C.1
How to implement stopping states

This can be done in a straightforward manner: given a stopping state of value
u\in[0,1]
, we consider a probability distribution that goes with probability
u
to a (self-looping) state
q_{\mathsf{A}}
of value 1 and that goes to a (self-looping) state
q_{\mathsf{B}}
of value 0 with probability
1-u
. Furthermore, note that the two corresponding BSCCs
\{q_{\mathsf{A}}\}
and
\{q_{\mathsf{B}}\}
(which exists regardless of the strategies of the players) behave properly w.r.t. the conditions for parity dominating a valuation (again, regardless of the strategies).

C.2
Markov chain induced by a pair of strategies

Definition 62

(Markov chain induced by a pair of positional strategies)
.

Consider a concurrent arena
\mathcal{C}
and two positional strategies
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}
(for Player
\mathsf{A}
) and
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}
(for Player
\mathsf{B}
). The Markov chain
\mathcal{M}_{\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}}=\langle Q,\mathbb{P}^{\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}}\rangle

induced
by
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}
is such that, for all
q\in Q
:

\forall q^{\prime}\in Q,\;\mathbb{P}^{\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}}(q)(q^{\prime}):=\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathbbm{1}_{q^{\prime}}\rangle}(\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}(q),\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))

C.3
Proof of Theorem
13

This theorem is straightforwardly deduced from already existing work, specifically,
[
3
]
and
[
5
]
. In particular, in this paper, results are stated by using the notion of MDPs and End Component. Informally, MDPs are games where only one Player plays. In particular, one obtains an MDP from a concurrent game by fixing the (positional) strategy of a player. An End Component
H
(EC for short) in an MDP is a set of states
Q_{H}\subseteq Q
such that one can ensure to stay in
Q^{\prime}
while the underlying graph of
H
if strongly connected. In particular, BSCCs are special cases of end components.

Proof
.

The first part of the theorem is given by
[
3
, Proposition 18]
. As for the second part, we apply
[
5
, Lemma 17]
. Indeed, the first condition is ensured by assumption. As for the second, consider a End Component
H
with
v_{H}>0
. This can be seen as a finite MDP — where only Player
\mathsf{B}
plays — with a parity objective. From
[
7
]
, Player
\mathsf{B}
has an optimal strategy
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}
that is positional and deterministic. Then, we obtain a Markov chain
H_{\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}}
where all BSCCs are compatible with the strategy
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}
(and the minimum of their values w.r.t. the valuation
v
is
v_{H}>0
). Hence, by assumption, all these BSCCs are even-colored, that is they have value 1. It follows that all states in
H_{\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}}
have value 1. Hence, the second condition of
[
5
, Lemma 17]
is ensured, and this lemma can be applied.

C.4
Proof of Lemma
21

Proof
.

The first equivalence comes from
[
4
, Lemma 16]
and the fact that positional deterministic strategies are enough (for Player
\mathsf{B}
) to play optimally in finite MDPs with parity objectives
[
7
]
. Now assume that
u>0
. Assume that the strategy
\mathsf{s}_{Y}^{\mathsf{A}}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}})
parity dominates
the valuation
v_{Y}^{u}
in the game
\mathcal{G}_{Y}
. In particular, it dominates this valuation, i.e. item
(ii.1)
of Lemma
21
is ensured. Consider now some action
b\in\mathsf{Act}_{\mathsf{B}}
such that
\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathcal{F},p(\cdot)[p_{[0,1]}]\rangle}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q_{0}))=0
and a Player-
\mathsf{B}
positional and deterministic strategy
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}
such that
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q_{0}):=b
. Since we have
\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathcal{F},p(\cdot)[p_{[0,1]}]\rangle}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q_{0}))=0
, no stopping state can be reached under
\mathsf{s}_{Y}^{\mathsf{A}}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}})
and
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}
. Consider the Markov chain
\mathcal{M}_{\mathsf{s}_{Y}^{\mathsf{A}}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}}),\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}}
. Besides stopping states, it is reduced to a BSCC
H
whose states are
q_{\mathsf{init}}
and all states
k_{i}
reachable with that action
b
. That is,
H=\{q_{\mathsf{init}}\}\cup\{k_{i}\mid i\in\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket,\;\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathcal{F},p(\cdot)(i)\rangle}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},b)>0\}
. Furthermore,
q_{\mathsf{init}}
is colored with
c
. The value of this BSCC
H
is in
\{0,1\}
and it is equal to
1
if and only if
H
is even-colored (since every state in a BSCC is almost-surely seen infinitely often). Since
u>0
, the value of
H
cannot be 0, thus
H
is even-colored and the maximum of the colors seen with that action is even. This exactly corresponds to item
(ii.2)
of Lemma
21
.

Assume now that the
\mathsf{GF}
-strategy
\sigma_{\mathsf{A}}
satisfies both items
(ii.1)
and
(ii.2)
. Consider a positional deterministic Player-
\mathsf{B}
strategy
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}
in the game
\mathcal{G}_{Y}
. Let
b:=\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q_{0})\in\mathsf{Act}_{B}
. Consider a BSCC
H
in the induced Markov chain
\mathcal{M}_{\mathsf{s}_{Y}^{\mathsf{A}}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}}),\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}}
that is not reduced to a stopping state. In particular, this implies that
\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathcal{F},p(\cdot)[p_{[0,1]}]\rangle}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},b)=0
. Then, as previously, we have
H=\{q_{\mathsf{init}}\}\cup\{k_{i}\mid i\in\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket,\;\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathcal{F},p(\cdot)(i)\rangle}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},b)>0\}
. The fact that
\sigma_{\mathsf{A}}
satisfies item
(ii.2)
ensures that the BSCC
H
is even-colored. It follows that the strategy
\mathsf{s}_{Y}^{\mathsf{A}}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}})
parity dominates the valuation
v_{Y}^{u}
.

C.5
Proof of Lemma
28

Proof
.

Consider such a strategy
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}
. Let us show that it parity dominates the valuation
\chi_{\mathcal{G}}
. First, note that it dominates the valuation
\chi_{\mathcal{G}}
since, for all
u\in V_{\mathcal{G}}\setminus\{0\}
, the strategy
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}^{u}
dominates
\chi_{\mathcal{G}}
in
\mathcal{G}^{u}
(recall that the stopping states in
\mathcal{G}^{u}
have the values of the original states in
\mathcal{G}
). Consider now a BSCC
H
compatible with
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}
such that
\min\chi_{\mathcal{G}}[H]>0
. By Theorem
13
, there is a value
u_{H}\in(0,1]
such that
\chi_{\mathcal{G}}[H]=\{u_{H}\}
. That is,
H\subseteq Q_{u_{H}}
. It follows that
H
is compatible with
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}^{u_{H}}
. Since
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}^{u_{H}}
parity dominates the valuation
\chi_{\mathcal{G}}
in
\mathcal{G}^{u_{H}}
, we can deduce that
H
is even-colored. Overall, the strategy
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}
parity dominates the valuation
\chi_{\mathcal{G}}
. By Theorem
13
, the strategy
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}
guarantees it (i.e. it is optimal).

Appendix D
Proof of Theorem
25

In this section, we give all the technical details used to prove Theorem
25
. The proof of this theorem is given in Page
6.5
, provided that Lemmas
44
,
47
,
48
and
49
hold. We already stated and argued why these lemmas hold, however we have not given a detailed proof of them. This is what we do in this section. However, we first state and prove results that we use in this section.

We first state and prove properties on the update of colors
\mathsf{NewCol}
. This is done in Page
D.1
. Then, we give the complete definition of faithfulness, see Page
D.2
. We also state and prove three lemmas, that relate local and global behaviors, see Page
D.3
. We finally prove the lemmas mentioned above: we prove Lemma
44
in Page
D.4
, Lemma
47
in Page
D.5
, Lemma
48
in Page
D.6
and Lemma
49
in Page
D.7
.

D.1
Properties ensured by the local operator
\mathsf{NewCol}

We introduce a useful notation which will allow us to rewrite Lemma
21
in our context.

Definition 63
.

Consider a state
q\in Q
, a virtual coloring function
\mathsf{vcol}:Q_{u}\rightarrow\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket
, a color
n\in\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket
and two
\mathsf{GF}
-strategies
(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{A}}(\mathsf{F}(q))\times\Sigma_{\mathsf{B}}(\mathsf{F}(q))
.
We denote by
\mathsf{Col}(q,\mathsf{vcol},\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},\sigma_{B})
the set of colors reachable in one step with positive probability w.r.t.
(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})
in the local game
\mathcal{G}_{q,\mathsf{vcol}}^{n}
(regardless of the color
n\in\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket
considered). That is:

\mathsf{Col}(q,\mathsf{vcol},\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},\sigma_{B}):=\{i\in\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket\mid\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathbbm{1}_{p_{q,\mathsf{vcol}}^{-1}[k_{i}]}\rangle}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})>0\}

In particular,
\mathsf{Col}(q,\mathsf{vcol},\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},b)=\mathsf{Color}(\mathsf{F}(q),p_{q,\mathsf{vcol}},\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},b)
for all
b\in\mathsf{Act}_{\mathsf{B}}
(notation from Lemma
21
).

Then, the set of colors
\mathsf{ColBSCC}(q,\mathsf{vcol},n,\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},\sigma_{B})
is defined by:

\mathsf{ColBSCC}(q,\mathsf{vcol},n,\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},\sigma_{B}):=\mathsf{Col}(q,\mathsf{vcol},\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},\sigma_{B})\cup\{\max(\mathsf{vcol}(q),c_{n})\}

with
c_{n}:=n-1
is
n
is even and
c_{n}:=n+1
is
n
is odd.

We obtain a corollary of Lemma
21
(which only consists in writing Lemma
21
in the context of a local game
\mathcal{G}_{q,\mathsf{vcol}}^{n}
).

Corollary 64
.

Consider a state
q\in Q
, a virtual coloring function
\mathsf{vcol}:Q_{u}\rightarrow\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket
, a color
n\in\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket
and a local strategy
\sigma_{\mathsf{A}}\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{A}}(\mathsf{F}(q))
. A Player-
\mathsf{A}

\mathsf{GF}
-strategy
\sigma_{\mathsf{A}}\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{A}}(\mathcal{F})
is

optimal
w.r.t.
Y:=(\mathsf{F}(q),E_{q,\mathsf{vcol}}^{n})
if and only if, letting

u:=\chi_{\mathcal{G}_{Y}}(q_{\mathsf{init}})
, either (i)
u=0
, or (ii)
the positional Player-
\mathsf{A}
strategy
\mathsf{s}^{Y}_{\mathsf{A}}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}})
parity
dominates the valuation
v_{Y}^{u}
.

Furthermore (ii) is equivalent to:

•

(1): the Player-
\mathsf{A}
positional strategy
\mathsf{s}^{Y}_{\mathsf{A}}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}})
dominates the valuation
v_{Y}^{u}
; and

•

(2): for all
b\in\mathsf{Act}_{\mathsf{B}}
, if
\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathcal{F},\mathbbm{1}_{p^{-1}[0,1]}\rangle}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},b)=0
, then
\max\mathsf{ColBSCC}(q,\mathsf{vcol},n,\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},\sigma_{B})
is even.

This is symmetrical for Player
\mathsf{B}
.

Let us now state a proposition we will use to prove that the update of colors cannot decrease the colors of the states.

Proposition 65
.

Let
q\in Q_{u}
and some virtual coloring function
\mathsf{vcol}:Q_{u}\rightarrow\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket
, some color
n\in\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket
and a positive value
z\in(0,1]
. Let
p:=p_{q,\mathsf{vcol}}
and
Y:=(\mathsf{F}(q),E_{q,\mathsf{vcol}}^{n})
. For all Player-
\mathsf{A}

\mathsf{GF}
-strategies
\sigma_{\mathsf{A}}\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{A}}(\mathsf{F}(q))
, the positional Player-
\mathsf{A}
strategy
\mathsf{s}_{Y}^{\mathsf{A}}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}})
dominates the valuation
v_{Y}^{z}
if and only if for all
b\in\mathsf{Act}_{\mathsf{B}}
: if
\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathbbm{1}_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}\rangle}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},b)>0
, then:

\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mu^{z}\rangle}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},b)\geq 0

where
\mu^{z}:Q\rightarrow[0,1]
is such that, for all
q\in Q
:

\mu^{z}(q):=\begin{cases}0&\text{ if }q\in Q_{u}\\
\chi_{\mathcal{G}}(q)-z&\text{ otherwise}\end{cases}

Proof
.

In the game
\mathcal{G}_{Y}
, the Player-
\mathsf{A}
strategy
\mathsf{s}_{Y}^{\mathsf{A}}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}})
dominates the valuation
v_{Y}^{z}
— property we denote
(1)
— if and only if:

\displaystyle z=v_{Y}^{z}(q_{\mathsf{init}})

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{val}{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),v_{Y}^{z}\rangle}(\mathsf{s}_{Y}^{\mathsf{A}}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}})(q_{\mathsf{init}}))

\displaystyle=\mathsf{val}{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),v_{Y}^{z}\rangle}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}})

In addition,
\mathsf{val}{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),v_{Y}^{z}\rangle}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}})=\min_{b\in\mathsf{Act}_{\mathsf{B}}}\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),v_{Y}^{z}\rangle}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},b)
. Furthermore, for all
b\in\mathsf{Act}_{\mathsf{B}}
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),v_{Y}^{z}\rangle}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},b)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathbbm{1}_{p^{-1}[\{q_{\mathsf{init}}\}\cup K_{e}]}\rangle}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},b)\cdot z

\displaystyle+\sum_{x\in V_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}}\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathbbm{1}_{p^{-1}[x]}\rangle}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},b)\cdot x

It follows that, if
\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathbbm{1}_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}\rangle}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},b)=0
, we have
\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),v_{Y}^{z}\rangle}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},b)=z
since
\cup_{x\in V_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}}p^{-1}[x]=Q\setminus Q_{u}
. However, if
\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathbbm{1}_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}\rangle}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},b)>0
, we have that

\displaystyle z\leq\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),v_{Y}^{z}\rangle}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},b)\Leftrightarrow 0

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{x\in V_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}}\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathbbm{1}_{p^{-1}[x]}\rangle}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},b)\cdot(x-z)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\sum_{x\in V_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}}\mathbbm{1}_{p^{-1}[x]}\cdot(x-z)\rangle}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},b)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mu^{z}\rangle}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},b)

The result follows.

The update of colors does not decrease the color.

Let us show that the local operator
\mathsf{NewCol}
does not decrease (w.r.t. the usual order
<
on natural numbers) the previous color of the state
q
given by a (virtual) coloring function
\mathsf{vcol}
.

Proposition 66
.

Consider a state
q\in Q_{u}
and a coloring function
\mathsf{vcol}:Q_{u}\rightarrow\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket
. We have
\mathsf{NewCol}(q,\mathsf{vcol})\geq\mathsf{vcol}(q)
.

Proof
.

We let
p:=p_{q,\mathsf{vcol}}
and, for all
n\in\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket
, we let
Y_{n}:=(\mathsf{F}(q),E_{q,\mathsf{vcol}}^{n})
. Note that
p_{[0,1]}\subseteq V_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}
.

There are two cases: either
\mathsf{vcol}(q)=e
or
\mathsf{vcol}(q)<e
. First, assume that
\mathsf{vcol}(q)=e
. Let us show that in that case
\mathsf{NewCol}(q,\mathsf{vcol})=e
. Assume towards a contradiction that
\chi_{\mathcal{G}_{Y_{e}}}(q_{\mathsf{init}})=u^{\prime}<u
for some
u^{\prime}\in[0,1]
. Consider a Player-
\mathsf{B}

\mathsf{GF}
-strategy
\sigma_{\mathsf{B}}
that is optimal w.r.t.
Y_{e}
. For all
a\in\mathsf{Act}_{\mathsf{A}}
, we have
\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathcal{F},\mathbbm{1}_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}\rangle}(a,\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})>0
. Indeed, otherwise, in the game
\mathcal{G}_{q,\mathsf{vcol}}^{e}
where Player
\mathsf{B}
plays the strategy defined by
\sigma_{\mathsf{B}}
, Player
\mathsf{A}
could loop indefinitely on
q_{\mathsf{init}}
thus ensuring winning with probability 1 (since the color of the state
q_{\mathsf{init}}
is
e
— as
\mathsf{vcol}(q)=e
— which is both the highest color appearing in the game and even). We let:

p_{\mathsf{Exit}}:=\min_{a\in\mathsf{Act}_{\mathsf{A}}}\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathcal{F},\mathbbm{1}_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}\rangle}(a,\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})>0

Now, consider some
a\in\mathsf{Act}_{\mathsf{A}}
. By Proposition
65
for Player
\mathsf{B}
, we have:

\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathcal{F},\mu^{u^{\prime}}\rangle}(a,\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})\leq 0
where
\mu^{u^{\prime}}:Q\rightarrow[0,1]
comes from Proposition
65
. Hence, we have:

\sum_{x\in V_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}}\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathcal{F},\mathbbm{1}_{Q_{x}}\rangle}(a,\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})\cdot x\leq\sum_{x\in V_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}}\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathcal{F},\mathbbm{1}_{Q_{x}}\rangle}(a,\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})\cdot u^{\prime}

Letting
v:=\chi_{\mathcal{G}}
be the value vector in the game
\mathcal{G}
, we have:

\displaystyle\sum_{q^{\prime}\in Q}\mathbb{P}^{q,q^{\prime}}{}(a,\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})\cdot v(q^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\sum_{q^{\prime}\in Q}\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathcal{F},\mathbbm{1}_{q^{\prime}}\rangle}(a,\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})\cdot v(q^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathcal{F},\mathbbm{1}_{Q_{u}}\rangle}(a,\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})\cdot u+\sum_{x\in V_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}}\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathcal{F},\mathbbm{1}_{Q_{x}}\rangle}(a,\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})\cdot x

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathcal{F},\mathbbm{1}_{Q_{u}}\rangle}(a,\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})\cdot u+\sum_{x\in V_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}}\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathcal{F},\mathbbm{1}_{Q_{x}}\rangle}(a,\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})\cdot u^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathcal{F},\mathbbm{1}_{Q_{u}}\rangle}(a,\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})\cdot u+\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathcal{F},\mathbbm{1}_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}\rangle}(a,\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})\cdot u^{\prime}

\displaystyle=u-\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathcal{F},\mathbbm{1}_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}\rangle}(a,\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})\cdot(u-u^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq u-p_{\mathsf{Exit}}\cdot(u-u^{\prime})<u=v(q)

Hence, letting
\delta:=p_{\mathsf{Exit}}\cdot(u-u^{\prime})/2>0
and considering, in the original game
\mathcal{G}
, a Player-
\mathsf{B}
strategy
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}\in\mathsf{S}_{\mathsf{B}}^{\mathcal{C}}
such that
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q):=\sigma_{\mathsf{B}}
and for all
q^{\prime}\in Q
, we have
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q\cdot q^{\prime})
a
\delta
-optimal Player-
\mathsf{B}
strategy from
q^{\prime}
, it follows that, for all Player-
\mathsf{A}
strategy
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}
in the game
\mathcal{G}
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{C},q}_{\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}}{}[W]

\displaystyle=\sum_{q^{\prime}\in Q}\mathbb{P}^{q,q^{\prime}}{}(\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}(q),\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})\cdot\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{C},q^{\prime}}_{\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}^{q},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}^{q}}{}[W]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{q^{\prime}\in Q}\mathbb{P}^{q,q^{\prime}}{}(\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}(q),\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})\cdot(v(q^{\prime})+\delta)\leq v(q)-2\delta+\delta=v(q)-\delta

where
W:=(\mathsf{col}^{\omega})^{-1}[W(\mathsf{col})]\subseteq Q^{\omega}
. Thus, the value from
q
is less than
u=v(q)
. Hence the contradiction. In fact,
\chi_{\mathcal{G}_{q,\mathsf{vcol}}^{e}}(q_{\mathsf{init}})\geq u
and
\mathsf{NewCol}(q,\mathsf{vcol})=e
.

Consider now the case where
\mathsf{vcol}(q)<e
. Assume towards a contradiction that
\mathsf{NewCol}(q,\mathsf{vcol})<\mathsf{vcol}(q)
. Let
n:=\mathsf{NewCol}(q,\mathsf{vcol})
.

Assume that
n
is even. By assumption, we have
\chi_{\mathcal{G}_{Y_{n}}}(q_{\mathsf{init}})\geq u
. Consider a Player-
\mathsf{A}

\mathsf{GF}
-strategy
\sigma_{\mathsf{A}}
that is optimal w.r.t.
Y_{n}
. First, the positional Player-
\mathsf{A}
strategy
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}:=\mathsf{s}_{Y_{n}}^{A}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}})=\mathsf{s}_{Y_{n+2}}^{A}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}})
defined by
\sigma_{\mathsf{A}}
dominates the valuation
v_{Y_{n}}^{u}=v_{Y_{n+2}}^{u}
in the game
\mathcal{G}_{Y_{n}}
and it also does in the game
\mathcal{G}_{Y_{n+2}}
. Consider now an action
b\in\mathsf{Act}_{\mathsf{B}}
and assume that

\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathcal{F},\mathbbm{1}_{p^{-1}[0,1]}\rangle}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},b)=0
. By Corollary
64
, it follows that, in the game
\mathcal{G}_{Y_{n}}
, we have
\max\mathsf{ColBSCC}(q,\mathsf{vcol},n,\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},b)
even with
\mathsf{ColBSCC}(q,\mathsf{vcol},n,\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},b)=\mathsf{Col}(q,\mathsf{vcol},\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},b)\cup\{e_{n}\}
with
e_{n}=\max(\mathsf{vcol}(q),c_{n})
and
c_{n}=n-1
since
n
is even. Since
n<\mathsf{vcol}(q)
, we have
e_{n}=\max(\mathsf{vcol}(q),c_{n})=\mathsf{vcol}(q)
. Furthermore,
e_{n+2}=\max(\mathsf{vcol}(q),c_{n+2})
with
c_{n+2}=n+1
since
n
is even. Since
n<\mathsf{vcol}(q)
, we also have
e_{n+2}=\mathsf{vcol}(q)=e_{n}
. That is,
\mathsf{ColBSCC}(q,\mathsf{vcol},n,\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},b)=\mathsf{ColBSCC}(q,\mathsf{vcol},n+2,\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},b)
. Hence,
\mathsf{ColBSCC}(q,\mathsf{vcol},n+2,\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},b)
is also even. By Corollary
64
, the Player-
\mathsf{A}

\mathsf{GF}
-strategy
\sigma_{\mathsf{A}}
strongly dominates the valuation
v_{Y_{n+2}}^{u}
. Hence, by Proposition
13
, the Player-
\mathsf{A}
positional strategy
\mathsf{s}_{Y_{n+2}}^{\mathsf{A}}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}})
guarantees the valuation
v_{Y_{n+2}}^{u}
. Hence the contradiction since this implies
\mathsf{NewCol}(q,\mathsf{vcol})\geq n+2
.

When
n
is odd, the reasoning is symmetrical, by taking the point-of-view of Player
\mathsf{B}
: we compare what happens in the games
\mathcal{G}_{Y_{m}}
and
\mathcal{G}_{Y_{m}}
for
m:=\mathsf{Succ}(n)
.

The update of colors is not affected by small changes of colors.

Let us now tackle another property ensured by the local operator
\mathsf{NewCol}
. Assume that the new color of a state
q
is
n\in\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket
w.r.t. a coloring function
\mathsf{vcol}
. Now, consider another coloring function
\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime}
that coincide with
\mathsf{vcol}
on colors at least
n
, and may differ for smaller colors. In that case, the new color of
q
will still be
n
w.r.t. the coloring function
\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime}
. This is (almost) what we prove here. First,
we introduce the notion of equivalent and prevailing coloring functions.

Definition 67

(Equivalent and Prevailing coloring functions)
.

Consider two coloring functions
\mathsf{vcol},\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime}:Q_{u}\rightarrow\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket
and some color
n\in\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket
. The coloring functions
\mathsf{vcol},\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime}
are
equivalent down to
n
if, for all
k\in\llbracket n,e\rrbracket
, we have
\mathsf{vcol}^{-1}[k]=\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime-1}[k]
.

Furthermore,
\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime}
is said to be
(n-1)
-
prevailing compared to

\mathsf{vcol}
if
\mathsf{vcol}
and
\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime}
are equivalent down to
n
and
\mathsf{vcol}^{-1}[n-1]\subseteq\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime-1}[n-1]
.

Let us first state a lemma that we will use to prove the proposition (of interest for us) that we state below.

Lemma 68
.

Consider a state
q\in Q_{u}
and some color
n\in\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket
. Let
\mathsf{vcol},\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime}:Q_{u}\rightarrow\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket
be two virtual coloring functions with
\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime}

n
-prevailing compared to
\mathsf{vcol}
. Consider a pair of
\mathsf{GF}
-strategies
(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})\in\Sigma_{\mathsf{A}}(\mathsf{F}(q))\times\Sigma_{\mathsf{B}}(\mathsf{F}(q))
. We have:

\displaystyle\max\mathsf{ColBSCC}(q,\mathsf{vcol},n,\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})\geq n\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\max\mathsf{ColBSCC}(q,\mathsf{vcol},n,\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})=\max\mathsf{ColBSCC}(q,\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime},n,\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})

Proof
.

Letting
c_{n}:=n-1
if
n
is even and
c_{n}:=n+1
if
n
is odd, we have (recall Definition
63
):

\mathsf{ColBSCC}(q,\mathsf{vcol},n,\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})=\mathsf{Col}(q,\mathsf{vcol},\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})\cup\{\max(\mathsf{vcol}(q),c_{n})\}

and

\mathsf{ColBSCC}(q,\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime},n,\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})=\mathsf{Col}(q,\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime},\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})\cup\{\max(\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime}(q),c_{n})\}

By assumption, we have that, for all
i\in\llbracket n+1,e\rrbracket
:

\mathsf{vcol}^{-1}[i]=\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime-1}[i]

Thus:

\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathbbm{1}_{p_{q,\mathsf{vcol}}^{-1}[k_{i}]}\rangle}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})=\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathbbm{1}_{p_{q,\mathsf{vcol}}^{-1}[k_{i}]}\rangle}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})

Furthermore:

\mathsf{vcol}^{-1}[n]\subseteq\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime-1}[n]

Thus:

\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathbbm{1}_{p_{q,\mathsf{vcol}}^{-1}[k_{n}]}\rangle}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})>0\Rightarrow\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathbbm{1}_{p_{q,\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime}}^{-1}[k_{n}]}\rangle}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})>0

We therefore obtain that for any
k\in\llbracket n,e\rrbracket
:

(i)

If
\max\mathsf{Col}(q,\mathsf{vcol},\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})\leq k
, then
\max\mathsf{Col}(q,\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime},\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})\leq k
;

(ii)

If
\max\mathsf{Col}(q,\mathsf{vcol},\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})=k
, then
\max\mathsf{Col}(q,\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime},\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})=k
.

Furthermore:

(i’)

If
\mathsf{vcol}(q)\leq k
, then
\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime}(q)\leq k
;

(ii’)

If
\mathsf{vcol}(q)=k
, then
\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime}(q)=k
.

We have three cases, letting
n\leq j:=\max\mathsf{ColBSCC}(q,\mathsf{vcol},n,\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})=\mathsf{Col}(q,\mathsf{vcol},\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})\cup\{\max(\mathsf{vcol}(q),c_{n})\}
:

•

If
j=c_{n}
, then
\max\mathsf{Col}(q,\mathsf{vcol},\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},\sigma_{\mathsf{B}}),\mathsf{vcol}(q)\leq j
. Hence, by (i) and (i’), we have
\max\mathsf{Col}(q,\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime},\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},\sigma_{\mathsf{B}}),\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime}(q)\leq j
.

That is,
\max\mathsf{ColBSCC}(q,\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime},n,\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})=j
.

•

If
j=\mathsf{vcol}(q)
then
\max\mathsf{Col}(q,\mathsf{vcol},\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},\sigma_{\mathsf{B}}),c_{n}\leq j
. Hence, by (ii’), we have
j=\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime}(q)
and, by (i), we have
\max\mathsf{Col}(q,\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime},\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})\leq j
.

That is,
\max\mathsf{ColBSCC}(q,\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime},n,\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})=j
.

•

If
j=\max\mathsf{Col}(q,\mathsf{vcol},\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})
then
\mathsf{vcol}(q),c_{n}\leq j
. Hence, by (ii), we have
j=\max\mathsf{Col}(q,\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime},\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})
and, by (i’), we have
\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime}(q)\leq j
. That is,
\max\mathsf{ColBSCC}(q,\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime},n,\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})=j
.

The lemma follows.

Proposition 69
.

Consider a state
q\in Q_{u}
and a coloring function
\mathsf{vcol}:Q_{u}\rightarrow\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket
. Let
n:=\mathsf{NewCol}(q,\mathsf{vcol})\in\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket
. Assume that another coloring function
\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime}:Q_{u}\rightarrow\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket
is
n
-prevailing compared to
\mathsf{vcol}
. In that case,
\mathsf{NewCol}(q,\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime})=n
.

Proof
.

Let us consider two such coloring functions
\mathsf{vcol}
and
\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime}
. We have
n=\mathsf{NewCol}(q,\mathsf{vcol})\in\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket
. Consider a Player-
\mathsf{A}

\mathsf{GF}
-strategy
\sigma_{\mathsf{A}}
that is optimal w.r.t.
Y
for
Y:=(\mathsf{F}(d),E_{q,\mathsf{vcol}}^{n})
. Let
Y^{\prime}:=(\mathsf{F}(d),E_{q,\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime}}^{n})
,
p:=p_{q,\mathsf{vcol}}
and
p^{\prime}:=p_{q,\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime}}
. Let us show that
n\preceq_{\mathsf{par}}\mathsf{NewCol}(q,\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime})
. This straightforwardly holds if
n=e-1
. Assume now that
n\neq e-1
. The Player-
\mathsf{A}
strategy
\mathsf{s}_{Y}^{\mathsf{A}}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}})
dominates the valuations
v_{Y}^{u}=v_{Y^{\prime}}^{u}
.

Consider an action
b\in\mathsf{Act}_{\mathsf{B}}
and assume that
\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathbbm{1}_{p^{-1}[0,1]}\rangle}(\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},b)=0
. By Corollary
64
, we have
j:=\max\mathsf{ColBSCC}(q,\mathsf{vcol},n,\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},b)
is even with
\max\mathsf{ColBSCC}(q,\mathsf{vcol},n,\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},b)\geq\max(\mathsf{vcol}(q),c_{n})
for
c_{n}=n-1
if
n
is even and
c_{n}=n+1
otherwise. Since
n-1
is odd, it follows that, in any case,
\max\mathsf{ColBSCC}(q,\mathsf{vcol},n,\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},b)\geq n
. Then, by Lemma
68
, we have
\max\mathsf{ColBSCC}(q,\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime},n,\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},b)=\max\mathsf{ColBSCC}(q,\mathsf{vcol},n,\sigma_{\mathsf{A}},b)
, which is even.
As this holds for all
b\in\mathsf{Act}_{\mathsf{B}}
, the
\mathsf{GF}
-strategy
\sigma_{\mathsf{A}}
strongly dominates the valuation
v_{Y^{\prime}}^{u}
, hence, by Theorem
13
, the value of the state
q_{\mathsf{init}}
in the game
\mathcal{G}_{Y^{\prime}}
is at least
u
:
\chi_{\mathcal{G}_{Y^{\prime}}}[q_{\mathsf{init}}]\geq u
. Hence,
n\preceq_{\mathsf{par}}\mathsf{NewCol}(q,\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime})
.

Let us now show that
\mathsf{NewCol}(q,\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime})\preceq_{\mathsf{par}}n
, which straightforwardly holds if
n=e
. Hence, assume that
n\neq e
and let
m:=\mathsf{Succ}(n)\in\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket
. The proof is very similar to the previous case. Let
Z:=(\mathsf{F}(d),E_{q,\mathsf{vcol}}^{m})
. Let also
Z^{\prime}:=(\mathsf{F}(d),E_{q,\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime}}^{m})
. The value of the state
q_{\mathsf{init}}
in the game
\mathcal{G}_{Z}
is at most
u^{\prime}
for some
u^{\prime}<u
:
\chi_{\mathcal{G}_{Z}}[q_{\mathsf{init}}]\leq u^{\prime}<u
. Consider a Player-
\mathsf{B}

\mathsf{GF}
-strategy
\sigma_{\mathsf{B}}
that is optimal w.r.t.
Z
. The Player-
\mathsf{B}
strategy
\mathsf{s}_{Z}^{\mathsf{B}}(\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})
dominates the valuation
v_{Z}^{u^{\prime}}=v_{Z^{\prime}}^{u^{\prime}}
. Consider an action
a\in\mathsf{Act}_{\mathsf{A}}
and assume that
\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathbbm{1}_{p^{-1}[0,1]}\rangle}(a,\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})=0
. We have
\max\mathsf{ColBSCC}(q,\mathsf{vcol},m,a,\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})
odd with
j:=\max\mathsf{ColBSCC}(q,\mathsf{vcol},m,a,\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})\geq\max(\mathsf{vcol}(q),c_{m})
for
c_{m}=m-1
if
m
is even and
c_{m}=m+1
otherwise. Let us show that
j\geq n
.

•

If
m
is odd (and
n=m+2
), we have
c_{m}=m+1=n-1
which is even. Hence,
j\geq n
.

•

If
m=0
(and
n=1
), since
j
is odd, it must be that
j\geq n
.

•

If
m\geq 2
is even (and therefore
n=m-2
), we have
c_{m}=m-1=n+1
. Hence, we have
j\geq n+1
.

We can apply Lemma
68
to obtain that
\max\mathsf{ColBSCC}(q,\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime},m,a,\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})=\max\mathsf{ColBSCC}(q,\mathsf{vcol},m,a,\sigma_{\mathsf{B}})
, which is odd. As this holds for all
a\in\mathsf{Act}_{\mathsf{A}}
, we have that the
\mathsf{GF}
-strategy
\sigma_{\mathsf{B}}
strongly dominating the valuation
v_{Z^{\prime}}^{u^{\prime}}
, hence, by Theorem
13
, the value of the state
q_{\mathsf{init}}
in the game
\mathcal{G}_{Z^{\prime}}
is at most
u^{\prime}
:
\chi_{\mathcal{G}_{Z^{\prime}}}[q_{\mathsf{init}}]\leq u^{\prime}
. Hence,
\mathsf{NewCol}(q,\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime})\prec_{\mathsf{par}}m
.

In any case, we have
n=\mathsf{NewCol}(q,\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime})
.

D.2
Complete definition of faithfulness

We want to formally give the definition of faithfulness that we will use in the proof. First, we define the notion of coherent coloring and environment functions. Informally, for each state
q\in Q_{u}
: the colors given by the coloring function correspond to the environment provided at each state (the environments being defined from coloring functions as in Definition
34
).

Definition 70

(Coherent coloring and environment functions)
.

Consider a virtual coloring function
\mathsf{vcol}:Q_{u}\rightarrow\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket
, some color
n\in\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket
and let
Q_{n}:=\mathsf{vcol}^{-1}[n]
. Consider an environment function
\mathsf{Ev}:Q_{n}\rightarrow\mathsf{Env}(Q)
and, for all
q\in Q_{n}
, let
\mathsf{vcol}_{q}:Q_{u}\rightarrow\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket
be the coloring function associated with the environment
\mathsf{Ev}(q)
. Let
q\in Q_{n}
. We say that
(\mathsf{vcol},\mathsf{Ev})
is
coherent
at state
q
if, letting
n_{q}:=\mathsf{NewCol}(q,\mathsf{vcol}_{q})
:

•

\mathsf{col}(q),\mathsf{vcol}_{q}(q),n_{q}\leq n
and the coloring function
\mathsf{vcol}
is
n
-prevailing compared to the coloring function
\mathsf{vcol}_{q}
;

•

n\equiv n_{q}\mod 2
and
\mathsf{Ev}(q)=\mathsf{CreateEnv}(n_{q},q,\mathsf{vcol}_{q})
where
\mathsf{CreateEnv}
corresponds to Algorithm
12
.

If this holds for all
q\in Q_{n}
,
(\mathsf{vcol},\mathsf{Ev})
is
coherent
at color
n
.

Definition 71

(Faithful pair of coloring and environment functions)
.

Consider a virtual coloring function
\mathsf{vcol}:Q_{u}\rightarrow\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket
, some
n\in\llbracket 0,e+1\rrbracket
and a partial environment function
\mathsf{Ev}:Q_{u}\rightarrow\mathsf{Env}(Q)
defined on
\mathsf{vcol}^{-1}[\llbracket n,e\rrbracket]
. We say that
(\mathsf{vcol},\mathsf{Ev})
is
faithful down to

n
if:

1f.

for all
k\in\llbracket n,e\rrbracket
, the pair
(\mathsf{vcol},\mathsf{Ev})
witnesses the color
k
;

2f.

for all
k\in\llbracket n,e\rrbracket
, the pair
(\mathsf{vcol},\mathsf{Ev})
is coherent at color
k
;

3f.

for all
q\in Q_{u}
, if
\mathsf{vcol}(q)<n
, then we have
\mathsf{col}(q)=\mathsf{vcol}(q)
and
\mathsf{NewCol}(q,\mathsf{vcol})<n
;

When
n=0
, we say that the pair
(\mathsf{vcol},\mathsf{Ev})
is
completely faithful
.

D.3
Three central lemmas

In the following, we state three lemmas that we will use in remainder of this chapter. The first lemma relates probability distributions in a local game and in a global game. This is particularly useful as it allows to use the assumptions made on the local strategies to obtain various properties on global games where such local strategies are used.

The second lemma states that any Player-
\mathsf{A}
positional strategy generated by an environment such that the new color (w.r.t. that environment) is even dominates a specific valuation. This is similar for Player
\mathsf{B}
.

Finally, the third lemma states that any Player-
\mathsf{A}
local strategy defined by local strategies that are optimal w.r.t. to an even color ensure that in a BSCC
H
compatible with such a strategy, if some high enough color occurs in
H
, then
H
is even-colored. This is similar for Player
\mathsf{B}
.

Lemma 72
.

Consider a virtual coloring function
\mathsf{vcol}:Q_{u}\rightarrow\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket
, some color
n\in\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket
and a partial environment function
\mathsf{Ev}:Q_{u}\rightarrow\mathsf{Env}(Q)
defined over
Q_{n}:=\mathsf{vcol}^{-1}[\{n\}]
. Let
q\in Q_{n}
and
\mathsf{vcol}_{q}:Q_{u}\rightarrow\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket
be the coloring function associated with environment
\mathsf{Ev}(q)
. We also let
p:=p_{q,\mathsf{vcol}_{q}}
. Then, for all Player-
\mathsf{A}
and Player-
\mathsf{B}
strategies
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}
and
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}
in the arena
\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{vcol}}^{n}
, we have:

\forall x\in V_{Q\setminus Q_{u}},\;\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{vcol}}^{n},q}^{\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}}[x]=\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathbbm{1}_{p^{-1}[x]}\rangle}(\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}(q),\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))

(1)

and

\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{vcol}}^{n},q}^{\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}}[Q_{n}\cup K^{n}]=\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathbbm{1}_{p^{-1}[\{q_{\mathsf{init}}\}\cup K_{e}]}\rangle}(\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}(q),\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))

(2)

Furthermore, if
\mathsf{vcol}
is equivalent down to
n+1
to
\mathsf{vcol}_{q}
:

\forall i\in\llbracket n+1,e\rrbracket,\;\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{vcol}}^{n},q}^{\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}}[k_{i}^{n}]=\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathbbm{1}_{p^{-1}[k_{i}]}\rangle}(\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}(q),\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))

(3)

and if, in addition,
\mathsf{vcol}
is
n
-prevailing compared to
\mathsf{vcol}_{q}
:

\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{vcol}}^{n},q}^{\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}}[\mathsf{vcol}_{q}^{-1}[n]\setminus\{q\}]=\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathbbm{1}_{p^{-1}[k_{n}]}\rangle}(\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}(q),\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))

(4)

Proof
.

By Definition
30
, we have:

•

for all
q^{\prime}\in Q_{n}
,
p_{n,\mathsf{vcol}}^{-1}[q^{\prime}]=\{q^{\prime}\}
;

•

for all
i\in\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket
:
p_{n,\mathsf{vcol}}^{-1}[k_{i}^{n}]=\mathsf{vcol}^{-1}[i]\setminus Q_{n}
;

•

for all
x\in V_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}
,
p_{n,\mathsf{vcol}}^{-1}[x]=Q_{x}
.

Furthermore, from Definition
34
, we have:

•

p^{-1}[q_{\mathsf{init}}]=\{q\}
;

•

for all
i\in\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket
:
p^{-1}[k_{i}]=\mathsf{vcol}^{-1}[i]\setminus\{q\}
;

•

for all
x\in V_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}
,
p^{-1}[x]=Q_{x}=p_{n,\mathsf{vcol}}^{-1}[x]
.

By Definition
31
, for all
x\in V_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}
. We have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{vcol}}^{n},q}^{\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}}(x)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathbbm{1}_{p_{n,\mathsf{vcol}}^{-1}[x]}\rangle}(\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}(q),\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))

\displaystyle=\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathbbm{1}_{p^{-1}[x]}\rangle}(\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}(q),\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))

This proves Equation (
1
).

Furthermore, using this Equation for the second equality, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{vcol}}^{n},q}^{\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}}[Q_{n}\cup K^{n}]

\displaystyle=1-\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{vcol}}^{n},q}^{\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}}[V_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}]

\displaystyle=1-\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathbbm{1}_{p^{-1}[V_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}]}\rangle}(\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}(q),\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))

\displaystyle=\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),1-\mathbbm{1}_{p^{-1}[V_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}]}\rangle}(\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}(q),\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))

\displaystyle=\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathbbm{1}_{p^{-1}[\{q_{\mathsf{init}}\}\cup K_{e}]}\rangle}(\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}(q),\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))

This proves Equation (
2
).

Assume now that
\mathsf{vcol}
is equivalent down to
n+1
to
\mathsf{vcol}_{q}
. Let
i\in\llbracket n+1,e\rrbracket
. We have
\mathsf{vcol}^{-1}[i]=\mathsf{vcol}_{q}^{-1}[i]
(recall Definition
67
). Furthermore, for all
q^{\prime}\in Q_{n}
, we have
\mathsf{vcol}(q^{\prime})=n\neq i
. Hence,
\mathsf{vcol}^{-1}[i]\setminus Q_{n}=\mathsf{vcol}^{-1}[i]=\mathsf{vcol}^{-1}[i]\setminus\{q\}
. Hence we have, again by Definition
31
, for all
i\in\llbracket n+1,e\rrbracket
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{vcol}}^{n},q}^{\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}}(k_{i}^{n})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathbbm{1}_{p_{n,\mathsf{vcol}}^{-1}[k_{i}^{n}]}\rangle}(\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}(q),\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))

\displaystyle=\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathsf{vcol}^{-1}[i]\rangle}(\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}(q),\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))

\displaystyle=\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathbbm{1}_{p^{-1}[k_{i}]}\rangle}(\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}(q),\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))

This gives Equation (
3
). Assume in addition that
\mathsf{vcol}
is
n
-prevailing compared to
\mathsf{vcol}_{q}
(recall Definition
67
). That is,
\mathsf{vcol}_{q}^{-1}[n]\subseteq\mathsf{vcol}^{-1}[n]=Q_{n}
. Hence,
\mathsf{vcol}_{q}^{-1}[n]\setminus\{q\}\subseteq Q_{n}
. It follows that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{vcol}}^{n},q}^{\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}}[\mathsf{vcol}_{q}^{-1}[n]\setminus\{q\}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{q^{\prime}\in\mathsf{vcol}_{q}^{-1}[n]\setminus\{q\}}\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathbbm{1}_{p_{n,\mathsf{vcol}}^{-1}[q^{\prime}]}\rangle}(\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}(q),\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))

\displaystyle=\sum_{q^{\prime}\in\mathsf{vcol}_{q}^{-1}[n]\setminus\{q\}}\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathbbm{1}_{q^{\prime}}\rangle}(\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}(q),\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))

\displaystyle=\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{vcol}_{q}^{-1}[n]\setminus\{q\}}\rangle}(\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}(q),\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))

\displaystyle=\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathbbm{1}_{p^{-1}[k_{n}]}\rangle}(\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}(q),\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))

We obtain Equation (
4
).

Lemma 73
.

Consider a virtual coloring function
\mathsf{vcol}:Q_{u}\rightarrow\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket
, some color
n\in\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket
and let
Q_{n}:=\mathsf{vcol}^{-1}[n]
. Consider an environment function
\mathsf{Ev}:Q_{n}\rightarrow\mathsf{Env}(Q)
. Assume that
(\mathsf{vcol},\mathsf{Ev})
is coherent at color
n
. In the arena
\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{vcol}}^{n}
:

•

if
n
is even, then all positional Player-
\mathsf{A}
strategies generated by the environment
\mathsf{Ev}
dominate the valuation
v_{n,\mathsf{vcol}}^{u}
in the arena
\mathcal{C}^{n}_{\mathsf{vcol}}
(see Definition
40
);

•

if
n
is odd, then there is some
y<u
such that, for all
z\geq y
, all positional Player-
\mathsf{B}
strategies generated by the environment
\mathsf{Ev}
dominate the valuation
v_{n,\mathsf{vcol}}^{z}
in the arena
\mathcal{C}^{n}_{\mathsf{vcol}}
(see Definition
40
).

Proof
.

For all states
q\in Q_{n}
, we let
\mathsf{vcol}_{q}:Q_{u}\rightarrow\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket
be the coloring function associated with the environment
\mathsf{Ev}(q)
and we let
p:=p_{q,\mathsf{vcol}_{q}}
. Let
q\in Q_{n}
. For all Player-
\mathsf{A}
and
\mathsf{B}
strategies
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}
in the arena
\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{vcol}}^{n}
, for all
z\in[0,1]
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),v_{n,\mathsf{vcol}}^{z}\rangle}(\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}(q),\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))

\displaystyle=z\cdot\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{vcol}}^{n},q}^{\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}}[Q_{n}\cup K]+\sum_{x\in V_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}}x\cdot\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{vcol}}^{n},q}^{\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}}(x)

Assume that
n
is even and consider a Player-
\mathsf{A}
positional strategy
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}
generated by the environment function
\mathsf{Ev}
. Letting
Y_{q}:=(\mathsf{F}(q),\mathsf{Ev}(q))
, in the game
\mathcal{G}_{Y_{q}}
, the positional Player-
\mathsf{A}
strategy
\mathsf{s}_{Y_{q}}^{\mathsf{A}}(\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}(q))
dominates the valuation
v_{Y_{q}}^{u^{\prime}}
for
u^{\prime}:=\chi_{\mathcal{G}_{Y_{q}}}[q_{\mathsf{init}}]\geq u
(recall Lemma
21
) since
(\mathsf{vcol},\mathsf{Ev})
is coherent at color
n
. Hence:

\displaystyle u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),v_{Y_{q}}^{u^{\prime}}\rangle}(\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}(q),\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))

\displaystyle=\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathbbm{1}_{p^{-1}[\{q_{\mathsf{init}}\}\cup K_{e}]}\rangle}(\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}(q),\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))\cdot u^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\sum_{x\in V_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}}\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathbbm{1}_{p^{-1}[x]}\rangle}(\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}(q),\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))\cdot x

\displaystyle=u^{\prime}\cdot\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{vcol}}^{n},q}^{\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}}[Q_{n}\cup\mathsf{K}^{n}]+\sum_{x\in V_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}}x\cdot\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{vcol}}^{n},q}^{\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}}[x]

Then, we have (since
u^{\prime}\geq u
):

\displaystyle u=u^{\prime}+(u-u^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq u^{\prime}\cdot\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{vcol}}^{n},q}^{\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}}[Q_{n}\cup K^{n}]+\sum_{x\in V_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}}x\cdot\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{vcol}}^{n},q}^{\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}}[x]+(u-u^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq u^{\prime}\cdot\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{vcol}}^{n},q}_{\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}}[Q_{n}\cup K^{n}]+\sum_{x\in V_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}}x\cdot\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{vcol}}^{n},q}^{\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}}[x]+(u-u^{\prime})\cdot\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{vcol}}^{n},q}^{\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}}[Q_{n}\cup K^{n}]

\displaystyle=u\cdot\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{vcol}}^{n},q}^{\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}}[Q_{n}\cup K^{n}]+\sum_{x\in V_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}}x\cdot\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{vcol}}^{n},q}^{\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}}[x]

\displaystyle=\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),v_{n,\mathsf{vcol}}^{u}\rangle}(\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}(q),\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))

Since this holds for all Player-
\mathsf{B}
positional strategies strategies
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}
and for all
q\in Q_{n}
, it follows that the Player-
\mathsf{A}
strategy
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}
dominates the valuation
v_{n,\mathsf{vcol}}^{u}
in the arena
\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{vcol}}^{n}
.

In the case where
n
is odd, the proof is analogous, from Player-
\mathsf{B}
’s point-of-view.

Lemma 74
.

Consider a virtual coloring function
\mathsf{vcol}:Q_{u}\rightarrow\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket
, some
n\in\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket
and
Q_{n}:=\mathsf{vcol}^{-1}[\{n\}]
. Consider an environment function
\mathsf{Ev}:Q_{n}\rightarrow\mathsf{Env}(Q)
and assume that
(\mathsf{vcol},\mathsf{Ev})
is coherent at the color
n
. Then, denoting
K^{\geq n}:=\{k_{i}^{n}\mid i\in\llbracket n,e\rrbracket\}
:

•

if
n
is even, then in the arena
\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{vcol}}^{n}
, all positional Player-
\mathsf{A}
strategies generated by the environment
\mathsf{Ev}
ensure that for all BSCCs
H
compatible with
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}
, if
K^{\geq n}
occurs in
H
, then
H
is even-colored;

•

if
n
is odd, then in the arena
\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{vcol}}^{n}
, all positional Player-
\mathsf{B}
strategies generated by the environment
\mathsf{Ev}
ensure that for all BSCCs
H
compatible with
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}
, if
K^{\geq n}
occurs in
H
, then
H
is odd-colored.

Proof
.

Assume that
n
is even and consider a Player-
\mathsf{A}
positional strategy
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}
generated by the environment
\mathsf{Ev}
in the game
\mathcal{L}^{n}_{\mathsf{vcol}}
(recall Definition
31
). Consider a Player-
\mathsf{B}
positional deterministic strategy
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}
and consider a BSCC
H
compatible with
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}
and
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}
.

For all
q\in Q_{n}
, let
\mathsf{vcol}_{q}:Q_{u}\rightarrow\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket
be the coloring function associated with the environment
\mathsf{Ev}(q)
. Let
q\in Q_{n}
and
n_{q}:=\mathsf{NewCol}(q,\mathsf{vcol}_{q})
. Since
(\mathsf{vcol},\mathsf{Ev})
is coherent at
n
,
n_{q}
is even and less than or equal to
n
. Recall that
\mathcal{G}_{q,\mathsf{vcol}}^{n_{q}}=\mathcal{G}_{Y_{q}}
, for
Y_{q}:=(\mathsf{F}(q),E^{n_{q}}_{q,\mathsf{vcol}_{q}})
(see Definition
34
). Recall also that
E^{n_{q}}_{q,\mathsf{vcol}_{q}}=\langle\max(c_{n_{q}},\mathsf{vcol}_{q}(q)),e,p_{\{q\},\mathsf{vcol}_{q}}\rangle
(also see Definition
34
) with
c_{n_{q}}=n_{q}-1
as
n_{q}
is even.
Let
p^{q}:=p_{q,\mathsf{vcol}_{q}}
. Note that
p^{q}_{[0,1]}\subseteq V_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}
(recall Definition
34
). By Equation
(\ref{eqn:prob_in_V_Q_Q_u_equal})
from Lemma
72
, if
q
is in
H
, then
\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathbbm{1}_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}\rangle}(\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}(q),\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))=0
. In that case, since the Player-
\mathsf{A}

\mathsf{GF}
-strategy
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}(q)
is optimal w.r.t.
Y_{q}
(recall Lemma
21
), we have the following.:

\max(\mathsf{Color}(\mathsf{F}(q),p^{q},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}(q),\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q)),n_{q}-1,\mathsf{vcol}_{q}(q))\text{ is even }

(5)

Since
(\mathsf{vcol},\mathsf{Ev})
is coherent (see Definition
70
) at
n
, we have
\mathsf{col}(q)\leq n
for all
q\in Q_{n}
. Hence, our goal is to show that:

K^{\geq n}\text{ occurs in }H\Rightarrow\max M\text{ is even }

with
M:=\{i\in\llbracket n,e\rrbracket\mid k_{i}^{n}\text{ occurs in }H\}
.

To prove this, we use the following characterization: for all colors
i\in\llbracket n+1,e\rrbracket
, by Equation
(\ref{eqn:prob_ki_if_equiv_equal})
in Lemma
72
since
\mathsf{vcol}
and
\mathsf{vcol}_{q}
are equivalent down to
n+1
, we have the following equivalence:

k_{i}^{n}\text{ occurs in }H\Leftrightarrow\exists q\in H,\;i\in\mathsf{Color}(\mathsf{F}(q),p^{q},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}(q),\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))

(6)

Furthermore, for all
q\in H
, by assumption (the pair
(\mathsf{vcol},\mathsf{Ev})
being coherent),
\mathsf{vcol}_{q}(q)\leq n
. Hence, since
n_{q}\leq n
, Equation (
5
) gives that, for all
q\in H
:

\max\mathsf{Color}(\mathsf{F}(q),p^{q},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}(q),\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))\geq n+1\Rightarrow\max\mathsf{Color}(\mathsf{F}(q),p^{q},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}(q),\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))\text{ is even }

(7)

Furthermore, if
\max M=n
, then
n
is the highest color appearing in
H
(since
\mathsf{vcol}
and
\mathsf{Ev}
are coherent at
n
) and
H
is then even-colored. Otherwise:

\displaystyle K^{\geq n}\text{ occurs in }H

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow M\neq\emptyset

\displaystyle\Rightarrow M\neq\emptyset\wedge k_{m}^{n}\text{ occurs in }H\text{ for }m:=\max M

\displaystyle\Rightarrow M\neq\emptyset\wedge\exists q_{m}\in H,\;m\in\mathsf{Color}(\mathsf{F}(q_{m}),p,\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}(q_{m}),\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q_{m}))

by Equation (
6
)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow M\neq\emptyset\wedge\exists q_{m}\in H,\;\max\mathsf{Color}(\mathsf{F}(q_{m}),p^{q_{m}},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}(q_{m}),\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q_{m}))=m

\displaystyle\text{ since }m=\max M\text{ and by Equation~(\ref{eqn:equiv_in_bscc}) }

\displaystyle\Rightarrow M\neq\emptyset\wedge m=\max M\text{ is even }

\displaystyle\text{ since }m\geq n+1\text{ and by Equation~(\ref{eqn:if_Iq_at_least_n_then_max_even}) }

We obtain the desired result.

The case of
n
odd is analogous by reversing the instances of ’odd’ and ’even’. However, the arguments for obtaining the analogue of Equation (
5
) is slightly different. Indeed, letting
Y_{q}:=(\mathsf{F}(q),E^{\mathsf{Succ}(n_{q})}_{\{q\},\mathsf{vcol}_{q}})
, the Player-
\mathsf{B}

\mathsf{GF}
-strategy
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q)
is optimal w.r.t.
Y_{q}
(recall Lemma
21
and the definition of
\mathsf{CreateEnv}
(i.e. Algorithm
12
)). Recall that
E^{\mathsf{Succ}(n_{q})}_{\{q\},\mathsf{vcol}_{q}}=\langle\max(c_{\mathsf{Succ}(n_{q})},\mathsf{vcol}_{q}(q)),e,\mathsf{vcol}_{q}\rangle
where
c_{\mathsf{Succ}(n_{q})}=-1
if
n_{q}=1
and
c_{\mathsf{Succ}(n_{q})}=n_{q}-1
otherwise (i.e. if
n_{q}\geq 3
). Overall, from Lemma
21
, we do obtain:

\max(\mathsf{Color}(\mathsf{F}(q),p,\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}(q),\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q)),n_{q}-1,\mathsf{vcol}_{q}(q))\text{ is odd }

(8)

with
n_{q}-1
even.

D.4
Proof of Lemma
44
, Page
44

Let us define a notion which is slightly weaker then being faithful: being non-deceiving.

Definition 75

(Non-deceiving environment and coloring functions)
.

Consider a virtual coloring function
\mathsf{vcol}:Q_{u}\rightarrow\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket
, a partial environment function
\mathsf{Ev}:Q_{u}\rightarrow\mathsf{Env}(Q)
and some color
n\in\llbracket 0,e+1\rrbracket
. We say that the pair
(\mathsf{vcol},\mathsf{Ev})
is
non-deceiving down to

n
if:

1n-d.

for all
k\in\llbracket n,e\rrbracket
, the pair
(\mathsf{vcol},\mathsf{Ev})
witnesses the color
k
;

2n-d.

for all
k\in\llbracket n,e\rrbracket
, the pair
(\mathsf{vcol},\mathsf{Ev})
is coherent at color
k
;

3n-d.

for all
q\in Q_{u}
, if
\mathsf{vcol}(q)<n
, then we have
\mathsf{col}(q)=\mathsf{vcol}(q)
and
\mathsf{NewCol}(q,\mathsf{vcol})\leq n
.

The difference with being faithful lies in the fact that some state
q\in Q_{u}
with
\mathsf{vcol}(q)<n
could be such that
\mathsf{NewCol}(q,\mathsf{vcol})=n
(which is not possible with a faithful pair).
When
n=0
, being non-deceiving down to
n
is equivalent to being faithful down to
n
(i.e. to being completely faithful).

Then, Algorithm
15
is composed of Algorithm
13
and Algorithm
14
. In fact, Algorithm
13
transforms a faithful pair into a non-deceiving one (one level below). And Algorithm
14
transforms a non-deceiving pair into a faithful one (at the same level). We state one lemma per algorithm formally stating the specifications of these algorithms.

Lemma 76
.

Consider a virtual coloring function
\mathsf{vcol}:Q_{u}\rightarrow\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket
, some color
n\in\llbracket 1,e+1\rrbracket
and a partial environment function
\mathsf{Ev}:Q_{u}\rightarrow\mathsf{Env}(Q)
defined on
\mathsf{vcol}^{-1}[\llbracket n,e\rrbracket]
. Assume that
(\mathsf{vcol},\mathsf{Ev})
is faithful down to
n
. Let
\mathsf{Ev}^{\prime}\leftarrow\mathsf{UpdCurSta}(n-1,\mathsf{vcol},\mathsf{Ev})
. Then,
(\mathsf{vcol},\mathsf{Ev}^{\prime})
is non-deceiving down to
n-1
.

Proof
.

For all
q\in Q_{u}
, such that
\mathsf{vcol}(q)\geq n-1
, we denote by
\mathsf{vcol}_{q}
the coloring function corresponding to the environment
\mathsf{Ev}^{\prime}(q)
.

2n-d.

Let us show that
(\mathsf{vcol},\mathsf{Ev}^{\prime})
is coherent at
n-1
. We let
Q_{n-1}:=\mathsf{vcol}^{-1}[n-1]
and
q\in Q_{n-1}
. Since
(\mathsf{vcol},\mathsf{Ev})
is faithful down to
n
, we have
\mathsf{vcol}(q)=\mathsf{col}(q)=n-1
. Furthermore,
\mathsf{vcol}_{q}=\mathsf{vcol}
. Hence,
\mathsf{vcol}_{q}(q)=\mathsf{col}(q)=n-1
. In addition, since the pair
(\mathsf{vcol},\mathsf{Ev})
is faithful down to
n
, we have
\mathsf{NewCol}(q,\mathsf{vcol})<n
. By Proposition
66
, we have
n-1=\mathsf{vcol}(q)\leq\mathsf{NewCol}(q,\mathsf{vcol})<n
. Hence,
\mathsf{vcol}(q)=\mathsf{NewCol}(q,\mathsf{vcol})=n-1
. By definition of Algorithm
12
, we have
\mathsf{Ev}^{\prime}(q)=\mathsf{CreateEnv}(n-1,q,\mathsf{vcol})
. As this holds for all
q\in Q_{n-1}
,
(\mathsf{vcol},\mathsf{Ev})
is coherent at
n-1
.

3n-d.

This condition straightforwardly holds since the coloring function has not changed and the pair
(\mathsf{vcol},\mathsf{Ev})
is faithful down to
n
.

1n-d.

This condition straightforwardly holds for
k\geq n
since the pair
(\mathsf{vcol},\mathsf{Ev})
is faithful down to
n
. Note that since
(\mathsf{vcol},\mathsf{Ev}^{\prime})
is coherent at
n-1
, both Lemma
73
and Lemma
74
can be applied to
(\mathsf{vcol},\mathsf{Ev}^{\prime})
at
n-1
.

Assume that
n-1
is even. Consider a Player-
\mathsf{A}
positional strategy
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}
generated by the environment
\mathsf{Ev}
in the game
\mathcal{L}^{n-1}_{\mathsf{vcol}}
. By Lemma
73
, this strategy dominates the valuation
v_{n-1,\mathsf{vcol}}^{u}
in the arena
\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{vcol}}^{n-1}
. Consider a Player-
\mathsf{B}
positional deterministic strategy
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}
and consider a BSCC
H
in
\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{vcol}}^{n-1}
compatible with
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}
and
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}
. By Lemma
74
, if
K^{\geq n-1}
occurs in
H
, then
H
is even-colored. Assume now that
K^{\geq n-1}
does not occur in
H
. In that case, there are some states in
H
in
Q_{n-1}\subseteq\mathsf{col}^{-1}[n-1]
and possibly some states in
\{k_{i}^{n-1}\mid i\in\llbracket 0,n-2\rrbracket\}
occur in
H
with
\mathsf{vcol}_{n-1}(k_{i}^{n-1})=n-2
for all
i\in\llbracket 0,n-2\rrbracket
and
\mathsf{vcol}_{n-1}[Q_{n-1}]=\{n-1\}
. Hence, the highest color appearing in
H
is
n-1
and therefore
H
is even-colored. Thus,
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}
parity dominates the valuation
v_{n-1,\mathsf{vcol}}^{u}
.

The case where
n-1
is odd is analogous, from Player-
\mathsf{B}
’s point-of-view.

Lemma 77
.

Consider a virtual coloring function
\mathsf{vcol}:Q_{u}\rightarrow\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket
, some color
n\in\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket
, and a partial environment function
\mathsf{Ev}:Q_{u}\rightarrow\mathsf{Env}(Q)
defined on
\llbracket n,e\rrbracket
. Assume that the pair
(\mathsf{vcol},\mathsf{Ev})
is non-deceiving down to
n
. Let
(\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime},\mathsf{Ev}^{\prime})\leftarrow\mathsf{UpdNewSta}(n,\mathsf{vcol},\mathsf{Ev})
. Then,
(\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime},\mathsf{Ev}^{\prime})
is faithful down to
n
.

Proof
.

For all
q\in Q_{u}
such that
\mathsf{vcol}(q)\geq n
, we denote by
\mathsf{vcol}_{q}
the coloring function associated with the environment
\mathsf{Ev}^{\prime}(q)
. Let
Q_{n}:=(\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime})^{-1}[n]
.

2f.

For all
k\in\llbracket n+1,e\rrbracket
, the pair
(\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime},\mathsf{Ev}^{\prime})
is coherent at
k
since the pair
(\mathsf{vcol},\mathsf{Ev})
is non-deceiving down to
n
. Now, let
q\in Q_{n}
. If
\mathsf{vcol}(q)=n
, then straightforwardly,
(\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime},\mathsf{Ev}^{\prime})
is coherent at state
q
since
(\mathsf{vcol},\mathsf{Ev})
is non-deceiving down to
n
, hence
\mathsf{vcol}
is
n
-prevailing compared to
\mathsf{vcol}_{q}
and
\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime}
is also
n
-prevailing compared to
\mathsf{vcol}
. Consider now a state
q\in Q_{n}
such that
\mathsf{vcol}(q)<n
. By definition of Algorithm
14
, we have
\mathsf{vcol}(q)=\mathsf{vcol}_{q}(q)\leq\mathsf{NewCol}(q,\mathsf{vcol}_{q})=n
(by Proposition
66
). That is,
\mathsf{vcol}_{q}(q)\leq n-1
. Since
(\mathsf{vcol},\mathsf{Ev})
is non-deceiving down to
n
, we have
\mathsf{col}(q)=\mathsf{vcol}(q)=\mathsf{vcol}_{q}(q)\leq n-1
. Furthermore, by definition of Algorithm
14
, the colors of the states in
\mathsf{vcol}^{-1}[\llbracket n,e\rrbracket]
is not changed and more and more states are colored with
n
. Hence,
\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime}
is
n
-prevailing compared to
\mathsf{vcol}_{q}
. In addition,
n=\mathsf{NewCol}(q,\mathsf{vcol}_{q})
and
\mathsf{Ev}(q)=\mathsf{CreateEnv}(n,q,\mathsf{vcol}_{q})
.
It follows that the pair
(\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime},\mathsf{Ev}^{\prime})
is coherent at state
q
, and this holds for all
q\in Q_{n}
.

3f.

By definition of Algorithm
14
, for all
k<n
and state
q\in Q_{u}
such that
\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime}(q)=k
, we have
\mathsf{NewCol}(q,\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime})\neq n
and
\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime}(q)=\mathsf{vcol}(q)=\mathsf{col}(q)
(since
(\mathsf{vcol},\mathsf{Ev})
is non-deceiving down to
n
). Furthermore, assume towards a contradiction that a state
q\in Q_{u}
is such that
\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime}(q)=k=\mathsf{vcol}(q)
and
\mathsf{NewCol}(q,\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime})=m>n
. In that case, by Proposition
69
, since
\mathsf{vcol}
and
\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime}
are equivalent down to
m
, we would have
\mathsf{NewCol}(q,\mathsf{vcol})=m>n
, which is not possible since
(\mathsf{vcol},\mathsf{Ev})
is non-deceiving down to
n
. In fact,
\mathsf{NewCol}(q,\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime})<n
.

1f.

Let
k\geq n+1
. We have
\mathsf{vcol}^{-1}[k]=(\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime})^{-1}[k]
and for all
q\in\mathsf{vcol}^{-1}[k]
, we have
\mathsf{Ev}(q)=\mathsf{Ev}^{\prime}(q)
. Hence, the first condition for faithfulness holds for
k
since
(\mathsf{vcol},\mathsf{Ev})
is non-deceiving down to
n
. We consider now the case where
k=n
. Assume that
n
is even. Consider a Player-
\mathsf{A}
positional strategy
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}
generated by the environment function
\mathsf{Ev}
in the game

. Let us show that this strategy parity dominates the valuation
v_{n,\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime}}^{u}
in the game
\mathcal{L}^{n}_{\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime}}
. By Lemma
73
, this strategy dominates the valuation
v_{n,\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime}}^{u}
. Now, fix a Player-
\mathsf{B}
positional deterministic strategy
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}
and consider a BSCC
H
compatible with
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}
and
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}
in
\mathcal{L}^{n}_{\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime}}
. If
K^{\geq n}
occurs in
H
, then by Lemma

, the BSCC

is even-colored.
Assume now that
K^{\geq n}
does not occur in

. Let

\forall i\in\llbracket 1,j\rrbracket,\;\mathsf{vcol}_{q_{i}}^{-1}[n]=X_{n}^{i-1}

(9)

First, assume that

. In that case, this is a BSCC compatible with the strategy

in the game

Assume towards a contradiction that

is

, which is odd. That is, the BSCC

his odd-colored, which is a contradiction. In fact,
\mathcal{P}(0)
holds.

Assume now that
\mathcal{P}(i)
holds for some

. Consider some state

. If

\mathsf{col}(q)\leq n-1

We have shown that

with

odd. Recall that

The case where

\mathsf{B}
strategy
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}^{\prime}
such that, for all
q\in S_{n}
,
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}^{\prime}(q):=\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q)
and for all
i\in\llbracket 0,n-1\rrbracket
, we have
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(k_{i}^{n})
playing in a deterministic way to reach
q
. By construction, we do have a BSCC
H^{\prime}
compatible with
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}
and
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{L}^{n}_{\mathsf{vcol}}
that is included in
Z\cup\{k_{i}^{n}\mid i\in\llbracket 0,n-1\rrbracket\}
. Furthermore, since the BSCC
H
in the game
\mathcal{L}^{n}_{\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime}}
is not equal to
H\cap S_{n}
, it must be that at least one state in
\{k_{i}^{n}\mid i\in\llbracket 0,n-1\rrbracket\}
is in
H^{\prime}
. Then, since each state in
\{k_{i}^{n}\mid i\in\llbracket 0,n-1\rrbracket\}
is colored with
n-1
and no state in
Z
is in
C_{n}
, it follows that the highest color in
H^{\prime}
is
n-1
, which is odd. That is, the BSCC
H^{\prime}
is odd-colored. This is a contradiction. In fact, from all states
q\in(H\cap S_{n})\setminus C_{n}
, we have
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime}}^{n},q}^{\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}}[(H\cap S_{n})^{*}\cdot C_{n}]>0
. Furthermore, the colors of all states in
H
are at most
n
(recall what we shown above in 2n-d). Hence, the highest color in
H
is
n
, which is even (the color of the states in
C_{n}
). Therefore the BSCC
H
is even-colored. That is, the Player-
\mathsf{A}
strategy
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}
parity dominates the valuation
v_{n,\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime}}^{u}
in the game
\mathcal{L}^{n}_{\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime}}
.

The case where
n
is odd is identical.

Hence, the pair
(\mathsf{vcol}^{\prime},\mathsf{Ev}^{\prime})
is non-deceiving down to
n
. The result then follows from Lemma
77
.

D.6
Proof of Lemma
48
, Page
48

Let us first introduce a way to build games with stopping states.

Definition 78
.

Consider a parity game
\mathcal{G}
. For all subsets of states
S\subseteq Q
, we denote by
\mathcal{G}^{S}
the game
\mathcal{G}
restricted to
S
where all states in
Q\setminus S
are stopping states with, for all
q\in Q\setminus S
,
q\leftarrow\chi_{\mathcal{G}}[q]
. (In particular, the set of states in
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{G}^{S}
is the same.)

For all
x\in[0,1]
, we also let
v_{S}^{x}:Q\rightarrow[0,1]
such that
\left.v_{S}^{x}\right|_{Q\setminus S}:=\left.\chi_{\mathcal{G}}\right|_{Q\setminus S}
and
v_{S}^{x}[S]:=\{x\}
.

Lemma 79
.

Consider a parity game
\mathcal{G}
and a subset of states
S\subseteq Q
. Then:
\chi_{\mathcal{G}}=\chi_{\mathcal{G}^{S}}
.

Proof
.

The equality straightforwardly holds for all
q\in Q\setminus S
. Consider now a state
q\in S
. Let
\varepsilon>0
and let
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}
be a Player-
\mathsf{A}
strategy that is
\varepsilon
-optimal from
q
in the game
\mathcal{G}
. This strategy can be seen as a strategy in the game
\mathcal{G}^{S}
, the game ending as soon as the set
Q\setminus S
is reached. Consider any Player-
\mathsf{B}
strategy
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}
in the game
\mathcal{G}^{S}
. Since the games
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{G}^{S}
coincide on
S^{*}
(and
S^{\omega}
) and considering a Player-
\mathsf{B}
strategy
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}^{\prime}
in the game
\mathcal{G}
that coincides with the strategy
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}
on
S^{*}
and that plays a
\varepsilon
-optimal strategy as soon as a state in
Q\setminus S
is reached. We have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}}_{\mathcal{C}^{S},q}[W(\mathsf{col})]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}^{\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}}_{\mathcal{C}^{S},q}[W(\mathsf{col})\cap S^{\omega}]+\sum_{q^{\prime}\in Q\setminus S}\mathbb{P}^{\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}}_{\mathcal{C}^{S},q}[S^{*}\cdot q^{\prime}]\cdot\mathbb{P}^{\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}}_{\mathcal{C},q^{\prime}}[W(\mathsf{col})]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}^{\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{C},q}[W(\mathsf{col})\cap S^{\omega}]+\sum_{q^{\prime}\in Q\setminus S}\mathbb{P}^{\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{C},q}[S^{*}\cdot q^{\prime}]\cdot\chi_{\mathcal{G}}[q^{\prime}]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}^{\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{C},q}[W(\mathsf{col})\cap S^{\omega}]+\sum_{q^{\prime}\in Q\setminus S}\mathbb{P}^{\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{C},q}[S^{*}\cdot q^{\prime}]\cdot(\mathbb{P}^{\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{C},q^{\prime}}[W(\mathsf{col})]-\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}^{\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{C},q}[W(\mathsf{col})]-\varepsilon\geq\chi_{\mathcal{G}}[q^{\prime}]-2\varepsilon

As this holds for all Player-
\mathsf{B}
strategies
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}
, it follows that.
\chi_{\mathcal{G}^{S}}[\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}](q)\geq\chi_{\mathcal{G}}(q)-2\varepsilon
. As this holds for all
\varepsilon>0
, we have
\chi_{\mathcal{G}^{S}}(q)\geq\chi_{\mathcal{G}^{S}}[\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}](q)\geq\chi_{\mathcal{G}}(q)
. By symmetry of the players, we have
\chi_{\mathcal{G}^{S}}(q)=\chi_{\mathcal{G}}(q)
.

We can proceed to the proof of Lemma
48
.

Proof
.

Assume towards a contradiction that
\min C=e-1
. Let
Q_{e-1}:=\mathsf{vcol}^{-1}[e-1]
. Consider a Player-
\mathsf{B}
strategy
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}
generated by the environment function
\mathsf{Ev}
. Because the pair
(\mathsf{vcol},\mathsf{Ev})
is completely faithful and
e-1
is odd, it follows that
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}
parity dominates the valuation
v_{e-1,\mathsf{vcol}}^{u^{\prime}}
in the game
\mathcal{L}^{e-1}_{\mathsf{vcol}}
for some
u^{\prime}\leq u
. Consider the game
\mathcal{G}^{Q_{e-1}}
from Definition
78
(we do not mention any player since the game has a value). Lemma
79
gives that all states have the same values in
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{G}^{Q_{e-1}}
. Let us show that there is some
x\in[0,1]
such that
x<u
and such that the strategy
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}
parity dominates the valuation
v_{Q_{e-1}}^{x}
in the game
\mathcal{G}^{Q_{e-1}}
. First, note that for all
y\in[0,1]
, if
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}
dominates the valuation
v_{Q_{e-1}}^{y}
, then it also parity dominates it (since
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}
parity dominates the valuation
v_{e-1,\mathsf{vcol}}^{u^{\prime}}
in the game
\mathcal{L}^{e-1}_{\mathsf{vcol}}
).

Now, consider some state
q\in Q_{e-1}
. Let
\mathsf{vcol}_{q}
be the coloring function associated with the environment
\mathsf{Ev}(q)
. Denoting
Y_{q}:=(\mathsf{F}(q),\mathsf{Ev}(q))
and
p^{q}:=p_{q,\mathsf{vcol}_{q}}
, we have that the Player-
\mathsf{B}

\mathsf{GF}
-strategy
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q)
is optimal w.r.t.
Y_{q}
. Hence, by Lemma
21
for Player
\mathsf{B}
, we have that for all Player-
\mathsf{A}
actions
a\in\mathsf{Act}_{\mathsf{A}}
, if
\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathcal{F},\mathbbm{1}_{(p^{q})^{-1}[p^{q}_{[0,1]}]}\rangle}(a,\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))=0
then
\mathsf{Color}(\mathsf{F}(q),p^{q},a,\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))\cup\{e-2\}
is odd with
\mathsf{Color}(\mathsf{F}(q),p^{q},a,\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q)):=\{i\in\llbracket 0,e\rrbracket\mid\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathbbm{1}_{(p^{q})^{-1}[k_{i}]}\rangle}(a,\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))>0\}
. Since

is even, it must be that, for all states

and

\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathcal{F},\mathbbm{1}_{(p^{q})^{-1}[p^{q}_{[0,1]}]}\rangle}(a,\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))=0\Rightarrow\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathbbm{1}_{(p^{q})^{-1}[k_{e}]}\rangle}(a,\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))=0

(11)

For

, let
\mathsf{NZ}(q):=\{a\in\mathsf{Act}_{\mathsf{A}}^{q}\mid\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathbbm{1}_{(p^{q})^{-1}[p^{q}_{[0,1]}]}\rangle}(a,\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))>0\}
. Then, we let:

p_{m}:=\min_{q\in Q_{e-1}}\min_{a\in\mathsf{NZ}(q)}\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathcal{F},\mathbbm{1}_{(p^{q})^{-1}[p^{q}_{[0,1]}]}\rangle}(a,\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))>0

and

x:=u+p_{m}\cdot(u^{\prime}-u)<u

(13)

Let us show that the strategy
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}
dominates the valuation
v_{Q_{e-1}}^{x}
in the game
\mathcal{G}^{Q_{e-1}}
. Let
q\in Q_{e-1}
. We have
\mathsf{val}{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),v\rangle}(\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))=\max_{a\in\mathsf{Act}_{\mathsf{A}}^{q}}\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),v\rangle}(a,\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))
. Consider some

and a Player-
\mathsf{A}

\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}
with
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}}(q):=a
. Denoting
P_{a}[T]:=\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathsf{vcol}}^{e-1},q}^{\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}}[T]
for any

, we have:

\displaystyle\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),v_{Q_{e-1}}^{x}\rangle}(a,\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))

\displaystyle=x\cdot\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathbbm{1}_{Q_{e-1}}\rangle}(a,\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))

\displaystyle+u\cdot\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathbbm{1}_{Q_{e}}\rangle}(a,\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))

\displaystyle+\sum_{y\in V_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}}y\cdot\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathbbm{1}_{Q_{y}}\rangle}(a,\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))

\displaystyle=x\cdot P_{a}[Q_{e-1}]+u\cdot P_{a}[\{k_{e}^{e-1}\}]+\sum_{y\in V_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}}y\cdot P_{a}[y]

In addition, since the strategy
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}
dominates the valuation

in the game

\displaystyle u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\geq\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),v^{u^{\prime}}_{e-1,\mathsf{vcol}}\rangle}(a,\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))

\displaystyle=u^{\prime}\cdot P_{a}[Q_{e-1}\cup\{k_{e}^{e-1}\}]+\sum_{y\in V_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}}y\cdot P_{a}[y]

That is:

By Equation (
1
) in Lemma
72
, for all
y\in V_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}
, we have:

P_{a}[y]=\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathbbm{1}_{(p^{q})^{-1}[y]}\rangle}(a,\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))

which can be applied since

, we have:

P_{a}[\{k^{e}_{e-1}\}]=\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathbbm{1}_{(p^{q})^{-1}[k_{e}]}\rangle}(a,\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))

Now, assume that
\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathbbm{1}_{(p^{q})^{-1}[V_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}]}\rangle}(a,\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))=0

y\in V_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}

. By Equation (

) it follows that

v_{Q_{e-1}}^{x}(q)=x=\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),v_{Q_{e-1}}^{x}\rangle}(a,\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))

\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),\mathbbm{1}_{(p^{q})^{-1}[V_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}]}\rangle}(a,\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))>0
(i.e.

). By Equation (

), it implies that

. It follows that, by Equation (

\displaystyle\mathsf{out}_{\langle\mathsf{F}(q),v_{Q_{e-1}}^{x}\rangle}(a,\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}(q))

\displaystyle=x\cdot P_{a}[Q_{e-1}]+u\cdot P_{a}[\{k_{e-1}^{e}\}]+\sum_{y\in V_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}}y\cdot P_{a}[y]

\displaystyle\leq u\cdot P_{a}[Q_{e-1}]+u\cdot P_{a}[\{k_{e-1}^{e}\}]+u^{\prime}\cdot P_{a}[V_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}]

\displaystyle=u\cdot(1-P_{a}[V_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}])+u^{\prime}\cdot P_{a}[V_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}]

\displaystyle=u+P_{a}[V_{Q\setminus Q_{u}}]\cdot(u^{\prime}-u)\leq u+p_{m}\cdot(u^{\prime}-u)

\displaystyle=x=v_{Q_{e-1}}^{x}(q)

As this holds for all
x\in Q_{e-1}
, it follows that the Player-
\mathsf{B}
strategy
\mathsf{s}_{\mathsf{B}}
dominates the valuation
v^{x}_{Q_{e-1}}
, and therefore parity dominates it. Hence, for all
q\in Q_{e-1}
, we have
\chi_{\mathcal{G}^{Q_{e-1}}}[q]\leq x<u
. That is a contradiction with Lemma
